# Upstream behavioral census (ADR-0008's durability mechanism)

Date: 2026-11-04. Upstream pin: `fc49368845` (the vendored submodule `gitea/`); re-graded for the RE-PIN campaign against `099abf5db4` — module 8's issue rows refreshed 2026-11-20 (REPIN-ISSUE); module 10's release rows refreshed 2026-11-20 (REPIN-RELEASE → the RELEASE2 resume); module 6's repo rows refreshed 2026-11-20 (REPIN-REPO); module 3's password rows + module 2's oauth2 rows refreshed 2026-11-20 (REPIN-OAUTH).
Companion to the 117-table structural census (`docs/research/table-alignment-audit.md`)
— that one measures schema presence against the pin; this one measures
**behavior** against the pin, per ADR-0008: *"upstream's implemented standard
behavior is IN SCOPE by default"* and *"the completion standard is behavioral
equivalence, not code presence — verified against upstream's same-input /
same-precondition outcomes."*

## 1. Methodology (the frame every module row follows)

Each censused surface is one graded row with four cells:

1. **Upstream behavior at the pin** — read from the pinned Go code (route,
   service, struct), with file:line cites. Never from feature names, release
   notes, or docs (ADR-0008 rule 6: judgments derive from the pin's actual
   behavior, per entry point — auth entrypoints especially, since web login,
   API Basic, and git-HTTPS each carry their own 2FA/PAT/password rules).
2. **Our implementation state** — the Worker-side module with file:line cites.
3. **Test evidence** — which landed suite pins the behavior (the suite is the
   regression harness the row rests on; a row without evidence says so).
4. **Grade** — the ADR-0008 re-graded vocabulary:

| Grade | Meaning | Stands or re-opens |
|---|---|---|
| **aligned** | Same input and precondition produce the same external outcome (API/Web-UI/Git-protocol/auth/permission/error semantics). | Stands. |
| **platform-adapted** | Internals differ (D1/R2/DO/WebCrypto/Queues replace Go store, app.ini, server session, cron) but external behavior is equivalent; divergence recorded with a **PLATFORM cite**. | Stands — with its cite, per ADR-0008 rule 5. |
| **strengthened / tightened** | Deliberate safe-direction delta (extra guard, refused legacy input, stronger replay guard) with a SPEC record. | Stands, listed here so the delta is visible in one place. |
| **deferred-reopened** | A recorded deferral (`deferred` / `out-of-scope` / `unsupported` / accepted-and-ignored) covering upstream-standard behavior. Per ADR-0008 rule 5 a deferred record is **not** a permanent exemption: it re-opens as backlog. | Re-opens → §21 backlog batch. |
| **not-yet-censused** | The surface exists in the module but no graded row yet. | Next census pass. |

Two hard rules applied while grading:

- **Silent ignores are contract defects** (ADR-0008 rule 4): any operation a UI
  or API accepts must really execute or explicitly error. Every
  accepted-and-ignored field found in this census is a `deferred-reopened` row,
  never "fine because documented".
- **Platform-adapted requires the cite to actually force the shape.** "We
  chose not to" is not a platform force; those rows re-open.

## 2. The 16-module list

The census is module-by-module; a module is COMPLETE when every surface it
owns carries a graded row with both-side cites, and its recorded divergences
from SPECs and the ledger have been re-graded through §1's vocabulary.

pass (CENSUS-M3FINAL, 2026-11-13, §20): **17 of the 17 rows COMPLETE — the
census is closed.**

## 3. Module 2 — AUTH beyond password (COMPLETE)

Scope: sessions, PAT scopes, TOTP flows, WebAuthn, OAuth2 provider + client,
OIDC/external sources, sudo, remember-me, rate limits. The password store and
the password sign-in matrix (web login, reset, admin-set, install init,
per-entry-point Basic password rules) are module 3's census (§20 — the pass
ran 2026-11-13); rows here cross-reference it and do not grade it.

### 3.1 Sessions and login plumbing

| # | Surface | Upstream at pin | Ours | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---------|-----------------|------|----------|-------|
| S1 | Web session store | Server-side session store behind the `gitea_session` cookie; ID regenerated at sign-in (`routers/web/auth/auth.go:333` `handleSignIn` → session insert). | `ui/session.ts` D1 row per session, sha256-at-rest secret, `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME = "gitea_session"` byte-compatible, 24 h TTL (`:189-198`). | `apps/worker/src/ui/session.test.ts` (route-level gate ladder, mint, clear set) | platform-adapted (PLATFORM: D1 replaces the server session store; cookie name and lifetime wire-equal) |
| S2 | Session rotation | Session ID regenerated at sign-in only; long-lived sessions keep one ID (`routers/web/auth/auth.go:333` regen path). | 24 h periodic rotation + 60 s stale grace so in-flight requests survive (`ROTATION_INTERVAL_MS`, `STALE_GRACE_MS`, `ui/session.ts:220-226`; `rotateSession` `:523`). | `ui/session.test.ts` rotation + forged-ticket arms | strengthened (recorded — SPEC-0010; strictly narrows the stolen-cookie window) |
| S3 | Remember-me | Separate long-lived cookie `CookieRememberName` carrying `tokenID:secret` (`routers/web/auth/auth.go:127`), validated by `CheckAuthToken` (`services/auth/auth_token.go:36-83`) with the 1-min rotated-cookie stale arm and the compromised-token revocation arm (old-hash use deletes the rotated token, `:66-73`); duration `setting.LogInRememberDays` (default off). | Same cookie carries an extended idle TTL on the ONE session row (`REMEMBER_IDLE_TTL_MS` 31 d, `REMEMBER_MAX_AGE_SECONDS`, `ui/session.ts:206-237`); no second token class. | `ui/session.test.ts` | platform-adapted for the single-cookie variant (PLATFORM: D1 session row carries the authority; upstream's two-token split is a store shape) — **but** the AuthToken stale/revocation semantics (`auth_token.go:60-73`) are behavior upstream implements that ours does not: re-opened as **B-11** |
| S4 | Sign-out | `routers/web/auth/auth.go:469-478` SignOut runs `ExitImpersonatedUser` against the raw session; session destroyed. | Four-directive clear set (valid, cookieless, forged, carriers-present) `ui/session.ts` via `deleteSessionBySecret`; dual-carrier clear on the 2FA path. | `ui/session.test.ts` logout arms | aligned |
| S5 | Prohibited account | API: bare `{message}` 403, no `url` field (`routers/api/v1/api.go:889-893`); git: plain-text 403 (SPEC-0003 row 11). | Same bare body on the middleware arm (`auth/middleware.ts`, pinned in `auth/middleware.test.ts`); SPEC-0022 §4.13 pins the two bare-body exceptions against every other arm's `{message, url}` wrapper. | `auth/middleware.test.ts` | aligned |
| S6 | Sudo (API) | `sudo()` (`routers/api/v1/api.go:111-140`): admin-only; unknown target → `APIErrorNotFound`; non-admin → 403 `"Only administrators allowed to sudo."`; audit `UserImpersonation` recorded; the admin stays attached as impersonator data. | `auth/middleware.ts` sudo arm; byte-compatible error bodies (SPEC-0002 §4.6 non-divergence list: "sudo semantics" kept byte-compatible). | `auth/middleware.test.ts` (admin/non-admin/missing-target), `auth/middleware.test.ts` bare-403 rows | aligned — one sub-check open: the API-sudo audit id (`UserImpersonation`) is not yet verified as emitted on our arm (our registered ids cover the WEB impersonation pair, SPEC-0022 §4.10) → **B-12** |
| S7 | Admin web impersonation | Session-data impersonator stamp; exit unconditional via `ExitImpersonatedUser` (`auth.go:469-478`). | `impersonateSessionUser` + exit-by-secret session (`ui/session.ts:628-746`); exit keys on (id, digest) + `impersonator_user_id` stamp so a prohibited/org target cannot strand the admin; audit pair `user:impersonation:start` / `:exit` emitted (`services/audit.ts` registry). | `ui/pages/admin-users-impersonate.test.ts`, `ui/session.test.ts` impersonation arms | platform-adapted (PLATFORM: exit authority moves from server session data to the stamped D1 row) + strengthened (prohibited-target reachability — SPEC-0022 §4.11) |
| S8 | CSRF / Origin guards | Per-form CSRF token machinery living in the request context (`services/context/context.go`), rendered by the template helpers as a per-form token. | `sameOriginGuard` on state-changing posts (`ui/session.ts:847`; oauth2 consent POST per `specs/oauth2-provider.md` §4.12) — token-free same-origin model. | `ui/session.test.ts` Origin guard; `oauth2-login.test.ts` | platform-adapted (PLATFORM: stateless Workers — no server-side token store to mint per-form tokens from; same-origin satisfies the same request-forgery threat) |
| S9 | Session GC | Session provider GC expires stale provider records (`modules/session/store.go:113-128` — the same store whose regeneration discipline SPEC-0022 §4.12 cites). | `sweepExpiredSessions` (`ui/session.ts:796`) on the cron state machine. | `ui/session.test.ts` | platform-adapted (PLATFORM: cron replaces provider GC) |

### 3.3 Two-factor (TOTP + WebAuthn) — SPEC-0022's surfaces

| # | Surface | Upstream at pin | Ours | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---------|-----------------|------|----------|-------|
| S25 | TOTP secret at rest | AES-encrypted with md5(SECRET_KEY), base64 stored (`models/auth/twofactor.go:93-106`). | base32 in the clear in D1; the in-flight copy travels only inside the AEAD ticket, never clear (`auth/two-factor.ts`). | `auth/two-factor.test.ts` AEAD confidentiality + rotation arms | platform-adapted (PLATFORM: no `[security] SECRET_KEY` exists to derive the AES key from — D1 is the source of truth; recorded SPEC-0022 §4.1) |

## 4. Module 6 — REPOS + SETTINGS (COMPLETE)

Scope: create (user/org/admin + push-create), fork, generate-from-template,
rename/redirect, transfer, archive, delete, visibility, the repo permission
model, and the settings surfaces (PATCH EditRepoOption, unit flags, merge
config, topics, mirror settings, wire counters). Git-HTTP transport rows live
in module 7's future census (SPEC-0003 §2.4 pre-verified them; they are
referenced, not re-graded here).

## 5. Module 4 — USERS + PROFILES (COMPLETE)

Scope: registration (incl. the email-verification flow), profile fields and
their visibility ladder, the settings surfaces (API + web), avatars, emails
CRUD, following/stars/blocks, the admin user pages' behavioral surface, and
the identity stores the family reads (keys). The password halves of
registration/admin-edit (store, hashing, complexity/pwned arms, install-init
arm) are module 3's matrix (graded §20) — rows here cross-reference it and do not
grade it, exactly as §3 does for S21/S22.

## 6. Module 7 — GIT-HTTP + SSH SURFACE (COMPLETE)

Scope: the git smart-HTTP protocol (the info/refs dance, upload-pack,
receive-pack's hooks and error reporting, the credential UX per entry point),
the archive endpoints (API + web), the SSH surface's posture, and the LFS
HTTP routes' behavioral contract. SPEC-0003 §2.4's ACL matrix (rows 1-14,
push-create 15-20, deploy tokens 21-23) was pre-verified in-tree; rows here
verify it stood and grade the surfaces the matrix does not cover. The
credential UX per entry point is §3's S-rows' territory (S19 realm, S20
token-forms dispatch, S21/S22 password+2FA arms, S24 deploy tokens, S51
failure limiter) — cross-referenced, not re-derived.

| # | Surface | Upstream at pin | Ours | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---------|-----------------|------|----------|-------|
| G1 | Route table + request parsing | `routers/web/githttp.go:10-23`: POST git-upload-pack / git-receive-pack / **git-upload-archive**, GET info/refs, GET HEAD, and the seven dumb-HTTP object routes; OPTIONS everywhere; `httpBase` service dispatch (`repo/githttp.go:62-85` — unknown service 400), the `Git-Protocol` header safety regex (`:339-341`), gzip request bodies (`:398-406`), CORS passthrough unless configured (`:48-58`). | `git-http/parse.ts` (route kinds, service resolution — `git-upload-archive` resolves to the bare 400 per `:234-241`, `SAFE_GIT_PROTOCOL_HEADER` `:253-257`), `gzip.ts`, `respond.ts` (`bareBadRequest:210-214` — the bodyless 400 shape); the dumb-HTTP object routes are NOT served (no `/objects/...` handler — the engine keeps objects under its own R2 layout). | `git-http/gate.test.ts`, `git-http/probe-cargo.test.ts` | aligned for the smart-HTTP table; the dumb-HTTP object family (service-less info/refs + objects/info/packs + loose object/pack/idx, `githttp.go:524-574`) is a mere deferral → **B-44**; git-upload-archive is a deferral → **B-43** (both legacy/secondary surfaces, no platform force — R2 could serve every shape) |

## 7. Module 8 — ISSUES + LABELS + MILESTONES (COMPLETE)

Scope: issue CRUD + the state machine, pinning, subscriptions, locking, labels
(repo + org + issue attach + scoped), milestones, assignees, dependencies and
blocks, comments (+ timeline + the deprecated routes), reactions, attachments,
issue templates + issue config, due dates, tracked time + stopwatches, the
search/list filter semantics, mentions, content history, and the deletion
cascade (PR rows ride the same issue row — the issue-delete row covers the PR
half). The webhooks the module emits are cross-referenced (module 13's
territory, `docs/compat/webhook-event-coverage.md`); SPEC-0005 §2.2's upstream
notes were re-derived here at the pin, not copied.

## 8. Module 9 — PULL REQUESTS + REVIEWS + MERGE (COMPLETE)

Scope: PR CRUD (a PR is an issue — §7's rows carry the shared arms), the
mergeability state machine, merge (all styles + the protection gate + the
automerge schedule + delete-branch-after + the message composition), reviews
(pending fold, official flags, dismissals, resolve arms), review requests,
commit statuses, diff/patch/files/commits reads, the update-branch route,
merge-upstream, the agit flow's posture, and the PR web faces. The push-side
branch-protection verdicts are §6 G4's rows; the D1 rule surface is
SPEC-0013's.

tags list page). Release webhook emission cross-references the event catalog
(module 13's territory, I24's precedent).

## 10. Module 11 — ACTIONS (COMPLETE)

Scope: the /api/v1 actions family (workflows list/get/disable/enable/
dispatch; runs get/delete/rerun-family/cancel-family/approve + attempts;
jobs + job logs; the tasks listing; artifacts list/get/delete/zip + the
signed raw download; runners CRUD + registration tokens; secrets and
variables — across repo/org/user/admin scopes), the runner-side
connect-RPC protocol (SPEC-0012's M1a/M1b — register, declare, fetch-task,
update-task, update-log), the artifacts upload protocols (v1/v4), the OIDC
issuer, the workflow dispatch machinery (inputs, ref resolution, triggers),
the badge, and the web faces (runs list, run viewer, logs, queue,
dispatch-inputs, workflow file, settings pages ×3 scopes, admin pages).
Upstream executes on EXTERNAL act_runner processes — the server is pure
control plane (`routers/api/actions/` is the RPC family; AGENTS.md's
SPEC-0012 note: "upstream's own architecture puts execution outside the
server — the control plane is pure parity work") — ours mirrors that shape;
the projects family's workflow routes (`api.go:779-798`,
`shared.ListProjectWorkflows` et al.) are the projects surface's territory
(uncensused) and out of scope here.

**RE-PIN delta (fc49368845 → 099abf5db4, REPIN-ACTIONS 2026-11-20)**: every
row's upstream cites re-derived at the new pin (the swagger annotation
refactor #40048 moved the whole `repo/action.go` surface; the error-system
refactors #40308/#40328 are wire-neutral). Behavioral deltas LANDED in the
worker tree this wave: #39961 dispatch runs post commit statuses
(A3/A17), #40106 pickup cancels Actions-disabled repos' jobs for every
runner scope (A12), #39942 the MaxJobNumPerRun insert ceiling + the
invalid-workflow run for rejected pushes (A17), #40392 evaluated
concurrency groups >255 runes hash to SHA-256 + `raw_concurrency` goes
TEXT (A17 — **SCHEMA HANDOFF: `action_run.raw_concurrency` and
`action_run_job.raw_concurrency` widen to TEXT, and the
`action_run_job`'s `is_reusable_caller` INDEX DROPS per #40055; migrations
are the orchestrator's staging**), #39998 the owner ActionsConfig corrupted-
value posture throws (the owner-actions-config store), #40336 the runners
settings Title becomes "Runners" (A15). HANDOFFS (not this unit's files):
the runner/variable audit arms (#40323) ride services/audit.ts +
routes/secrets-variables.ts + routes/admin-actions.ts + services/secrets-variables.ts;
the runner/variables settings PAGES' cite refreshes (user-settings-*,
repo-settings-* runners/secrets-variables, admin-actions-runners/-variables,
actions-scoped-workflows, actions-approve-all) and ui/index.ts's 36 drifted
cites; repo-actions-logs.ts / repo-actions-workflow.ts /
repo-actions-artifacts-download.ts (+their tests) kept their own pages-family
drift rows; services/pull commit-status PR-count filter (#39961's
`GetLatestPullCommitStatuses`) stages with the PR checks surface.

## 12. Module 12 — PACKAGES (COMPLETE)

Scope: the owner-scoped package model + content-addressed blob store, the
management API (the nine `/api/v1/packages` ops + the admin list), the 22
CommonRoutes registry protocol families plus the container OCI API (all 23
landed per SPEC-0016's milestone ladder), publish/download semantics (the
duplicate-version/immutable-vs-overwrite arms per type, the semver flags, the
download counters), the metadata/index arms (the distro index builders, the
cargo index + its git mirror, the signing keys), quota checks + the cleanup
arm (rules CRUD + execution + the unreferenced sweep), permissions (owner
visibility, org-team modes, the token-scope gate, the require-sign-in-view
arm B-34 recorded), and the web faces (R04 list/versions/view/settings, R16
repo tab, the owner-settings rule pages, the admin list). The `package`
webhook emissions cross-reference module 13 (WH4; the event-level audit is
`docs/compat/webhook-event-coverage.md`'s row, not duplicated here). SPEC-0016
owns the model/protocol SPECs; rows re-graded through §1's vocabulary — the
SPEC's ratified reductions (the 512 MiB generic quota, the org accessMode
collapse) re-opened where rule 5 requires.

## 18. Module 16 — API-V1 OVERALL SURFACE (COMPLETE)

Scope: the cross-cutting API machinery — the error envelope, the pagination
convention, the group scope-gate table, the unregistered-path posture, the
API documentation surface — plus the `miscellaneous` family (version, the
option-template trio, markup/markdown, the token lifecycle, the settings
quartet, the signing-key pair, the health check) that no per-route module
owns. The per-route families themselves are modules 2–15's graded rows
(cross-referenced from §2's COMPLETE cells). `docs/compat/
api-remaining-inventory.md` is STALE (surprise 6) and this census re-derives
from routes, not from it: its remaining-work list dissolves into this
section and the board.

pair, the gated API stubs family, and the retracted data plane. Upstream's

arm; `routers/api/v1/activitypub/person.go` is the entire package — one

(the pre-stub registration shape, as-built 2026-11-01, the user's
completion directive; the ledger's ADR-0007 §2 record), with the delivery
lane OQ-5-gated. The S-1 follower store is ledger history — this section
carries the graded rows.

## 20. Module 3 — AUTH PASSWORD MATRIX (COMPLETE)

Scope: the whole password system at the pin — the users-table store, the hash
service (both pbkdf2 arms + the salt eras), the shared Authenticate ladder
with its rehash-on-login migration, every entry point (web login, API Basic,
git-HTTPS), the reset machinery (time-limit code + reset-complete + the
forgot posture), change-password, the admin-set arms (API create/edit, the
web edit; the web-create page), install-init, the must-change column and its
two enforcement surfaces, and the policy module. The as-built record is
SPEC-0002 §6 (ADR-0008's first application); the landed Phase A is
09ea3f1/e5e7c4e/50dce36 (+ the PWD-2 enforcement wave §6.3.1) — graded here
2026-11-13 (CENSUS-M3FINAL), the census's final module pass. The §3
cross-references this census held for it (S21/S22/S23, the B-14 gate) close
below. Out of scope here, cross-referenced: registration's password face
(module 4's U1 row — the B-1 form-shape record), the external-source password
walk (B-18/B-19/B-20), the mail half of the reset lane (NF11, B-84), the
remember-me carrier (S3), the second-factor ceremonies (§3.3's rows —
SPEC-0022's surfaces). The Phase-B benchmark (`docs/research/hash-bench-phase-b.md`, PWD-3)
supplies this module's platform truths: dynamic wasm compilation is
hard-blocked, production Workers cap PBKDF2 at 100,000 iterations (local
workerd does not), upstream-default scrypt is unreachable at any knob — the
benchmark's verify-parity harness (§2.4, workerd == Node == OpenSSL/Node-wasm
== python3-bcrypt) is what makes the cost numbers below upstream's cost.

THE WIRE: the gate parses the request sections WITHOUT buffering the pack (`splitReceivePackSections` — commands, the NUL-suffix caps, the push-options section verbatim iff negotiated, the pack re-served as a stream), rebuilds the forward (translated `refs/pull/N/head` commands, rejects dropped, caps ride the first kept line, options + pack pass through), and rewrites the response in the CLIENT's command order (`rewriteReceivePackResponse`): the engine's report re-renders against the ORIGINAL `refs/for` names, synthesized ng lines splice in order, report-status-v2 clients (client-caps-driven) get upstream's option-line GROUPS (cmd/hook.go:604-620 — ok, `option refname` (the translated ref), `option old-oid` only when non-zero, `option new-oid`, `option forced-update` off the engine's force classification; upstream's `option fall-through` marker has no counterpart here — our fall-through commands are genuinely executed and reported by the engine's own report, not relayed through a hook), quiet clients (no report-status) get NO report but still carry the band-2 `hookPrintResult` frames (upstream prints to stderr regardless), an all-rejected push synthesizes its whole report without touching the engine, and the hookPrintResult message (agit.go:208/304's `%s/pulls/%d` — `repo.HTMLURL()` in upstream, our appOrigin chain's APP_URL-var-first precedence) rides band 2 first-class. The gate's lane validator wraps the shared push policy + the R5/D-7c collector with the force gate (an engine-classified force push ngs with agit.go's exact `request `force-push` push option` unless the option allowed it). THE TAILS (upstream ran them inside ProcReceive's wall clock; ours run inline post-response, before the client reads the report): create fill (title/description the options didn't carry derive from the tip commit — GetCommit fallback) + the `pull_request` action=opened webhook + the create action row; update re-arms headSha/status/divergence CAS + `pull_request_sync` synchronized with before/after oids (Update, agit.go:296-330); a failed create sweeps its pre-allocated rows (awaited before the client sees the ng). THE BOUNDARY (recorded, not silent): a `refs/for/*` push at a ROWLESS (engine-only) repo keeps the engine's literal ref — agit is a D1-repository behavior. The unified engine (services/codesearch/codesearch.go:49-102): ParseSearchQuery FIRST (modules/indexer/query.go — whitespace-tokenized terms, `"phrases"` with `\"`/`\\` escapes (empty → "Empty quoted value", post-quote text → "Unexpected text after closing quote", unterminated → "Unterminated quote"), `/regexp/` terms (1..256 bytes, `\/` decodes, invalid compile → "Invalid regexp"), path:/language:/repo:/user:/org: qualifiers (negated → "Negated qualifiers are not supported"; content:/symbol:/is:/in:/filename:/extension: → "Unsupported qualifier"; empty value → "Empty qualifier value"; repeated path/language → "Repeated … qualifier"; path leading-/ or `..` segment or unquoted `*?[` → "Invalid path qualifier", path.Clean'd, "." invalid; repo: must be OWNER/NAME; language via enry alias, unknown → "Unknown language"), LF/NUL → "Terms must not contain LF or NUL", bare AND/OR/NOT → "Boolean operators are not supported", >256-byte term → "Term exceeds 256 bytes", >16 terms → "Query exceeds 16 terms", zero terms → "Query must contain a search term"), then the per-engine rejections IN ORDER ("Regular expressions are not supported by the code indexer" on the Indexer engine when a term is non-literal; "Language qualifiers are not supported by git grep" on GitGrep+language:; "Repository and owner qualifiers are only supported in global code search" when repo:/user:/org: ride RepoID/OwnerName-scoped searches) — a QUALIFIER-ONLY query carries zero terms, so the parse-time no-term 422 wins over the engine rejections (pinned). gitgrep.PerformSearch (modules/indexer/code/gitgrep/gitgrep.go): GetCommit(ref || HEAD; empty repo → zero results, NO error), ListFiles = `ls-tree --full-tree -l -r` filtered by parseGitLsTreeOutput's IsRegular||IsExecutable (blobs only — symlinks/submodules never index) AND internal.IsSearchable (size > MaxIndexerFileSize 1 MB default → skip; ExcludeVendored=true + analyze.IsVendor → skip — the go-enry v2.9.6 VendorMatchers list minus the .gitignore/.gitattributes/.gitmodules basename override, modules/analyze/vendor.go:16-26; Include/ExcludePatterns default EMPTY), the `git grep -l -z -I --perl-regexp --ignore-case --full-name --all-match` prefilter ∪ single-term pathMatches, then the AUTHORITATIVE FileMatcher (internal/util.go MatchFile: EVERY term must match path OR content case-insensitively — literal substring in ToLower(content) with the lower→original range walk (util.go:126-141, the stale-boundary quirk on mid-form match starts) or NFC path contains; regexp per-line FindAllStringIndex capped 1000/file or NFC path match), 30 s deadline → incomplete, the 50-file MaxResultLimit cap → incomplete, util.PaginateSlice(page, pageSize default 50), total = len(results) (≤ 50). respondCodeSearch (code_search.go:34-76): GetListOptions (page max(,1), ToCorrectPageSize ≤ 50 default 30), the 1000-window guard 422 "page * limit must not exceed 1000" BEFORE Search (so ref 404 → window 422 → parse 422 is the arm order), codeSearchError maps SearchQueryError → 422 {message} / ErrIndexerUnavailable → 503 / else APIErrorInternal 500, item filtering by apiRepos[repoID] != nil (CanRead(TypeCode)), repo-scoped sets result.Repos = {RepoID: ctx.Repo.Repository} so item.repository = the FULL convert.ToRepo render, SetLinkHeader(min(total, 1000/limit*limit)) + SetTotalCountHeader(total), 200 {total_count, incomplete_results, items} with items never null. ToCodeSearchResultItem (services/convert/code_search.go): name = path.Base, url = repo.APIURL + "/contents/" + PathEscapeSegments(path) + "?ref=" + QueryEscape(commitID), html_url = repo.HTMLURL + "/src/" + RefNameFromCommit(commitID).RefWebLinkPath() (= "commit/<sha>") + "/" + PathEscapeSegments(path), language omitempty (the git-grep engine never sets it — the key is ABSENT), text_matches = BuildResultFragments(content, matches) (indexer/code/search.go:113-166 — max 10 blocks, 4 KiB TruncateStringBytes clips, the two-newline window, the merge arm when a match reaches into the previous block) each {object_url: url, object_type: "FileContent", property: "content", fragment, matches: [{text, indices: UTF-8 BYTE offsets in the fragment}]}. | LANDED 2026-11-20 (CODESRCH2): `api/routes/code-search.ts` (the pair, operationIds repoSearchCode/codeSearch, tags repository, q optional-declared so the absent param reaches the parser's no-term 422 — never a validation 400 — and a stray path URL param is dropped like Go's unknown params) + `api/services/code-search.ts` (the ParseSearchQuery port with the exact messages; the FileMatcher; IsSearchable with the FULL fetched go-enry v2.9.6 vendor list; BuildResultFragments; the scan over plain tree/blob engine reads — the pin's git-grep arm needs NO indexer, so the real-read IS the upstream posture) + `api/models/code-search.ts` (the four swagger definitions field-for-field) + the router.ts mount after registerContentsRoutes. The un-warmed case is the EXACT upstream posture: no code indexer exists → RepoIndexerEnabled=false → the global arm 404s behind reqToken's 401 (anonymous first — the api.go ladder), and the 503 ErrIndexerUnavailable arm is unreachable behind the gate (recorded, not ignored). Recorded divergences (each upstream-motivated, in-code): (1) NO git binary — the `--all-match` prefilter is cost-only (the matcher is the arbiter on both sides; the prefilter ∪ pathMatches ⊆ our full eligible scan yields the SAME result set), replaced by MAX_SCANNED_FILES/MAX_TREE_DIRS budgets → incomplete (the budget-exit-never-null rule; upstream's analog is the 30 s deadline); (2) JS per-code-point toLowerCase vs Go unicode.ToLower (exotic folds can shift a mapped range start, never the match decision); (3) RE2-only regexp syntax falls back to case-sensitive (unreachable upstream — parse validates); (4) the NUL-in-8KiB binary sniff (the landed isBinaryData idiom) for Go's typesniffer, non-text keeps path eligibility with empty content (ReadFile's nil posture); (5) `charset.ToUTF8DropErrors` = TextDecoder's lossy decode; (6) match/fragment offsets compute in JS string space and convert to UTF-8 BYTE offsets once at the wire (toWireIndices). reqRepoReader(TypeCode) is VACUOUS here (the issue-unit-gates.ts reduction — visibility IS the code-unit read; no 403 arm exists). THE SCOPE HANDOFF (scope-gate occupied): `/repos/…/code/search` rides the EXISTING `/^\/repos/` repository row; `/search/code` needs a NEW row `{pattern: /^\/search\/code(?:\/|$)/, categories: ["repository"], cite: "api.go:1897 (/search/code)"}` in SCOPE_GROUP_ROWS BEFORE nothing (no conflicts — no other row matches /search) — until it lands, a wrongly-scoped token answers the 404 where upstream 403s (the only ordering gap, flagged in the route header). **Batch total: 78 re-graded items (≥ 15 gate met).** Composition: 17 were
accepted-and-ignored or accepted-but-inert inputs (rule-4 violations — the
class ADR-0008 calls worse than absence; B-48/B-49/B-50 joined from the
modules 8/9 pass, B-62 from the modules 12/13 pass, B-79/B-81 from the
modules 16/17 pass, B-85 from the module-3 pass — the accepted-but-
unexecutable `pbkdf2_hi` parse), 56 mere deferrals
(B-54..B-57 joined from the modules 10/11 pass, B-58..B-61 + B-63/B-64 from
the modules 12/13 pass, B-65..B-67 + B-70..B-72 from the modules 14/15 pass,
B-73..B-77 from the modules 1/5 pass, B-78/B-80 from the modules 16/17 pass,
B-82/B-83/B-84/B-86/B-87/B-88 from the module-3 pass),
5 splits where a sibling arm genuinely
stands (B-22's reverse-proxy/SSPI, B-15's platform note is the QR deferral
itself, B-38's default-closed posture standing with the disabled-arm shape
re-opening, B-68's git/queue-sync ops and B-69's ChangeConfig/TestCache
standing with their cites from the modules 14/15 pass). **Surprises of the sweep**, in order of severity:

1. **The `generate` booleans lie (B-29).** `GenerateRepoOption` carries seven
   booleans; ours honors `git_content` and accepts the other six while
   returning success — the request semantics ("copy my topics/hooks/labels")
   are silently unmet. The rule-4 class, live in a shipped route.
2. **`object_format_name` sha256 is accepted and silently discarded (B-24).**
   The schema enum advertises sha256; the writer hardcodes sha1. The repo's
   own OpenAPI contract promises what the storage does not deliver.
3. **The stale docstring (R27).** `api/models/repository.ts` still documents
   the five mirror fields as accepted-and-ignored three waves after the route
   started applying them — proof the census's "read the code, not the
   comment" rule (ADR-0008 rule 6) earns its keep.
4. **`branch_count`'s fix is a table, not a walk (B-26).** Upstream's number
   is a COUNT over a push-time-maintained cache table — the exact D1 artifact
   — so the "always 0" divergence closes with schema-lane work, and the
   parity-review's `language ""` neighbor stays a separate engine question.
5. **`require-sign-in-view` was spec'd and never landed (B-34).** SPEC-0003's
   ACL matrix carries row 6; the config surface it keys on does not exist —
   the spec row is currently dead letter.
6. **`api-remaining-inventory.md` is stale.** It predates the Actions
   landing; module 16's census must re-derive from routes, not from it.
   (It did — §18 re-derived from the route code; the stale inventory's
   remaining-work list dissolves into §18/§21.)
7. **Upstream has NO rate limiting at the pin.** Our failure limiter and
   login rate limit are pure enhancements — nothing upstream-side to align
   with; they stand as strengthened rows without platform cites.
8. **The settings PATCH swallows oversized and non-URL input (B-35).**
   Upstream's `UserSettingsOptions` binding layer 422s a 101-char full_name
   and a non-URL website (`user.go:90-99`); ours stores both silently — the
   rule-4 class again, this time on the user's own account surface
   (modules 4/7 sweep).
9. **The heatmap counts the wrong source (B-40).** Upstream aggregates the
   `action` table (pushes included); ours reads issues + comments created —
   a push-only contributor renders an empty contribution graph. The right
   store is already landed (activity-feeds).
10. **Stale docstrings repeat (B-39/B-41).** `routes/blocks.ts:60` claims
    blocking is wired into NO path while the issue + follow arms landed and
    test-pinned (stale in both directions); `user-settings.tsx:70` still
    says "no avatar pipeline" three batches after the R2 pipeline landed.
    R27's lesson was not a one-off: reduction notes outlive their premises.
11. **git-HTTP config postures are rendered as constants (B-45).** The
    settings API serves `http_git_disabled: false` as a literal while no
    gate reads anything — the B-34 pattern (spec'd-or-known config surface
    with no implementing var) twice over, plus the dumb-HTTP and
    upload-archive absences (B-43/B-44) upstream still serves.
12. **The issues module's biggest absence is invisible from the API surface
    (B-46/B-47).** Cross-reference comment rows simply never exist — yet the
    timeline reader is fully built for them (the renderability predicate,
    the metadata render arms, even a manual-row test pin). An API consumer
    sees zero errors and zero xrefs; the gap is discoverable only by reading
    upstream's write paths (modules 8/9 sweep).
13. **Issue create drops the assignee fields it accepts (B-48).** The
    docstring honestly names it "the file-header deviation", but the schema
    advertises `assignee`/`assignees` and the create never applies them for
    writers — the rule-4 class on the module's front door. Everything else
    (PATCH arms, the assignee trio, PR create's assignee twin) is live.
14. **PR create drops the reviewer fields it accepts (B-49).** Same class,
    one route over: `reviewers`/`team_reviewers` bind and vanish, while the
    standalone review-request ops they would call are landed and
    test-pinned — the fix is a two-line call.
15. **PR PATCH's base retarget binds and vanishes (B-50).** "Engine-gated
    upstream" was recorded as the deferral reason, but the engine moves refs
    for every sibling arm (create/merge/update/delete) — the gate is a
    choice, not a force.
16. **`merge-upstream`'s 404 premise outlived the fork landing (B-51).**
    "Forks cannot exist yet" — they landed with the fork wave (R20); the
    always-404 stub and two docstrings kept arguing with the codebase.
17. **SPEC-0013 §6's auto-merge line is superseded (docs-only).** "The 409 on
    `merge_when_checks_succeed` stands" — the schedule landed with the FN-7
    `merged_commit_id` column and a working consumer; the census row P12
    records the supersession, and the SPEC's §6 list should shed the line at
    its next edit (recorded here so the stale-marker rule has a cite).
18. **Every pushed tag carries a releases-table row upstream (B-54 — LANDED
    2026-11-04).** The is_tag records are not API-side bookkeeping — the
    post-receive tail mints one per added tag (pushUpdateAddTags) and the API
    tag create inserts one (CreateNewTag). Ours minted none until the mint
    arms landed (the board row carries the as-built, incl. the push-create
    forward root-cause find); the visible deltas (ghost-row delete arms, the
    tags-page ordering universe) had been papered over with substitutes the
    in-file notes honestly named.
19. **"Latest release" is created_at-desc, not semver (RL5).** The brief's
    own assumption was wrong at the pin: `GetLatestReleaseByRepoID` orders
    `created_unix DESC, id DESC` over the non-draft/non-prerelease filter —
    no version comparison exists. Ours rides the identical predicate; the
    install-script contract is safe (modules 10/11 sweep).
20. **The dead-link class recurs on the web write tails (B-55/B-56).**
    Release generate-notes + the attachment upload/remove POSTs and the
    actions approve-all-checks arm all engine-404 while their API-side (or
    service-side) machinery is landed and test-pinned — the same shape as
    surprise 12, one store over.
21. **Actions secrets sat in D1 as plaintext (B-57 — LANDED 2026-11-04).**
    Upstream encrypts secret data with the instance key; ours stored the raw
    bytes until the versioned AEAD envelope landed (the board row carries
    the as-built; the wire was never affected). The S25 contrast and the
    B-64 twin (the webhook Authorization header, same class) are on the
    board.
22. **The packages codec/digest tails were economics notes, not platform
    forces (B-58).** Upstream renders `Packages.xz` beside the .gz baseline
    and MD5 digest lines/sections across the distro index builders; ours
    omits both with an economics record — while the same tree already ships
    hand-built zstd and bz2 decoders (the conda/composer landings), the
    existence proof that xz and MD5 are workerd-viable (modules 12/13 sweep).
23. **The org-team package grant WAS collapsed to one axis (B-59 — LANDED
    2026-11-13).** Upstream reads each team's `TypePackages` unit mode; ours
    folded every team to `teams.access_mode` — the resume unit landed the
    per-unit read seam over the existing `teams.units_map` (NO migration
    was needed; the parked "columns migration" framing was a misnomer):
    `teamPackagesUnitMode` + the blanket-write guard, upstream's
    package.go:142 composition, pinned in model.test.ts's B-59 describe.
24. **The package deleted-arm delivers nothing upstream-side (B-60).** Every
    upstream registry deleted endpoint (npm unpublish, the nuget/rubygems/
    chef/terraform version deletes, conan, the distro file-delete folds, the
    container tag delete) and the web package-settings deletes fire the
    `PackagePayload` deleted event; ours covers the v1 management pair + the
    generic cascade only — the coverage doc's own remaining-work item 1
    (modules 12/13 sweep).
25. **The `matrix` webhook type is bind-rejected and every provider rides
    the default request (B-61) — LANDED 2026-11-13 (B-61).** Upstream accepts
    11 hook types and ten requesters reshape the delivery; ours accepted 10
    (`models/webhook.ts:77-88` — `matrix` absent, so the zod enum 400s a type
    upstream accepts) and delivered the gitea json/form/GET request for every
    type — the refusal is the wrong-status contract-defect class, not an
    ignore (modules 12/13 sweep). LANDED: `matrix` joins `HOOK_TYPES` (the
    vendored swagger's 10-value declaration stays frozen — upstream's own
    doc/runtime split) and all nine provider requesters reshape the delivery
    under `webhooks/providers/` (the full transport + payload construction;
    see the B-61 row for the per-provider shapes and the two recorded
    platform adaptations — the APP_URL var and the reduced telegram
    sanitizer).
26. **The Actions subscription tail was accepted-and-ignored (B-62) — LANDED
    2026-11-04 (WHWAVE).** `updateHookEvents` now sets both flags by plain
    membership (upstream's utils/hook.go arms), `ALL_EVENTS` is all 26, the
    stale "deferred with the Actions surface" docstring retired, and the web
    settings matrix carries the Workflow Events checkbox pair (settings.tmpl
    :337-349) — the emitter needed no touch (it already delivered the pair).
27. **The notification surface is a full substitution, not a reduction
    (NF2).** Upstream materializes a row per recipient per issue at activity
    time; ours derives the whole thread set at read time and materializes
    only the read/pin state — and every observable arm (thread identity,
    statuses, ordering, the recipient union incl. the mute rule) is
    test-pinned equal anyway. The route module carries its own deviations
    doc, the SPEC-grade record the census usually has to write for it
    (modules 14/15 sweep).
28. **The mail fan-out landed 13 of 16 receivers — and the three missing
    are exactly the ones whose firing points live in OTHER modules' write
    paths (release publish, transfer request, actions run settle).** The
    absence is invisible from inside the mail module; only a receiver-count
    against the upstream interface list finds it (B-65, modules 14/15 sweep).
29. **The actions email preference is a writer without a reader (B-65).**
    The settings tab stores it (migration 0034) and nothing consumes it —
    the inverse of the usual accepted-and-ignored shape (a reader without a
    writer), which is why the rule-4 sweep did not catch it (modules 14/15
    sweep).
30. **The email-outbound SPEC's transport premise is stale as a platform
    fact (B-66).** "Workers cannot open outbound TCP" predates the
    platform's connect() API; the census's platform-cite discipline — the
    cite must actually force the shape — applies to PLATFORM claims inside
    SPECs too, so the SMTP arm re-opens while sendmail (exec) genuinely
    stands (modules 14/15 sweep).
31. **The admin audit-logs page was omitted for a reason that stopped being
    true (B-70).** "No landed audit-log store" — the audit_event store
    (SPEC-0015) and the user settings-audit-logs viewer landed afterwards;
    the monitor's note is stale in the proven direction. The stale-note
    class's fourth recurrence (R27, B-39, B-41, now the monitor) (modules
    14/15 sweep).
32. **The unadopted-repos family is the census's first case where
    "structurally empty" CLOSES a surface (AD3).** Every upstream arm
    (adopt.go's six) converges on the 404/204 pair only reachable from a
    filesystem state workerd cannot have — the degenerate API IS upstream's
    answer, so the row stands platform-adapted instead of re-opening
    (modules 14/15 sweep). *(Superseded by the 2026-11-20 re-pin: 1554b6c939
    deleted the adopt feature upstream outright, and the degenerate arm set
    went with it — the lesson stands, its conclusion moved to
    removed-with-upstream; AD3's row carries the re-grade.)*
33. **Bot users are a deliberate admin-created account class, not a shim
    (B-72).** The missing type value silently voids a whole arm family —
    the admin token management, the impersonate bot-refusal, the
    notification recipient skip (currently vacuous by our own deviation
    note) — the class-wider-than-the-row lesson (modules 14/15 sweep).
34. **The `user:create` audit id is absent while `organization:create` is
    emitted (B-73).** Upstream emits user:create on every creation path

35. **The health endpoint diverges in path AND payload (B-74).** `/healthz`

36. **The team feeds arm's honest-empty reason stopped being true (B-76).**
    "No activity model exists" — the same store now serves the repo/org/user

37. **Two more stale docstrings, each contradicted by a landed sibling in

38. **The attachment allow-list is published by the settings API and
    enforced by no arm (B-81) — LANDED 2026-11-13 (SMALLS2).** The settings quartet renders upstream's
    34-extension default verbatim while the upload POST's declared 422 is

    re-validation — the B-45 class (rendered constants, no gate) recurring

39. **`/markup`'s FilePath is accepted and discarded (B-79).** The schema

40. **The API documentation surface is absent while the tree's whole
    contract discipline is built on its substrate (B-80).** The vendored

41. **The unregistered-path 404 bytes differ from upstream's (B-78) —

    `404 Not Found`, upstream `404 page not found` — recorded honestly at

43. **A stored `pbkdf2_hi` row passes every local test and dies mid-verify in

44. **The reset flow lacks upstream's wrong-user refusal (B-83).** Upstream

45. **U22 cited the admin web create page it never graded (B-88).** The row

46. **The admin-edit password docstrings outlived their own posture (PW17).**
    Three comment blocks still describe the pre-PWD-2 disabled-with-notice

## 22. Completion protocol + the re-opened backlog lane

**Protocol** (each future module pass follows it): survey the upstream module
at the pin from the route/struct/service code (never feature names); enumerate
our implementation surfaces with file cites; attach the suite that pins each
row; grade through §1's vocabulary; sweep the module's SPEC/ledger deferred
markers through rule 5 (platform-forced keep, deferrals re-open onto §21's
table); tally and mark the module COMPLETE only when no row lacks a cite.
Landing review = the commit point; the census grows module rows in place.

**Standing verdicts this pass already carries for later modules** (graded
evidence landed with earlier units; cited so the next pass does not re-litigate):
- Git-HTTP ACL matrix rows 1–14 + push-create 15–20 + deploy-token 21–23:
  SPEC-0003 §2.4, verified in-tree at the pin's cites (module 7's census,
  §6 G5).
- Federation follower store: S-1 (ledger) landed, delivery lane OQ-gated
  (module 17's census, §19 FD4–FD8 — the graded rows supersede this verdict;
  upstream has no table to port — the architecture never mainlined,
  `#36789 1592576fa5` deleted the inbox arm).
- Wiki patch-series mirror / ghost-interim / gitlink / merge-template /
  squash-body arms: D-1..D-6 all LANDED per the ledger (module 9's census,
  §8 P7).
- The password matrix: module 3's pass ran (§20, CENSUS-M3FINAL, 2026-11-13)
  — 16 graded rows + 1 docs-defect record; the S21/S22/S23 cross-references
  and the B-14 gate this census held for it are closed by it.
- The issues/PR family's webhook payloads: I24's cross-reference — module
  13's census grades the catalog, `docs/compat/webhook-event-coverage.md`
  carries the event-level audit.

**Module 3 (mandate numbering 1–16)**: COMPLETE (§20, CENSUS-M3FINAL,
2026-11-13 — the protocol's last pass; its in-flight owner marker had been
cleared 2026-11-13 after the v1.5.8 freeze, when no unit claimed the
derivation). Modules 16–17 completed under the protocol above (CENSUS-M16,
2026-11-13, §18/§19); module 3 closed the census (CENSUS-M3FINAL, 2026-11-13,
§20): the table stands **17/17 COMPLETE — the census is closed.** The backlog
table (§21) is the census's output contract: items graduate to "landed" rows
only when a graded row replaces them, never by editing the deferral marker in
place (ADR-0008 rule 5).
